+  // Removes everything about a cloned `.el` that exists only for the EDITOR's benefit: selection/
+  // multi-select/blocked-flash outlines and the resize/rotate/connector-endpoint handles. Deliberately
+  // does NOT try to sanitize `.txt .body` here ??exportElementHTML() below throws that subtree away
+  // entirely and rebuilds it from normalized data instead of trying to launder the live markup, see
+  // its own comment for why.
+  function stripEditorChrome(clone) {
+    clone.classList.remove('selected', 'editing', 'multi-selected', 'blocked');
+    var handles = clone.querySelectorAll('.handle');
+    for (var i = 0; i < handles.length; i++) handles[i].remove();
+  }
+
+  /* Phase 6 REPAIR: a File -> its `data:<mime>;base64,...` URL, unlike fileToBase64() below (which
+   * strips that prefix for the server's pure-base64 field) -- an exported <img>/<video> `src` wants
+   * the prefix so the browser opening the standalone file knows how to decode it. */
+  function fileToDataURL(file) {
+    return new Promise(function (resolve, reject) {
+      var reader = new FileReader();
+      reader.onload = function () { resolve(String(reader.result || '')); };
+      reader.onerror = function () { reject(reader.error || new Error('could not read the file')); };
+      reader.readAsDataURL(file);
+    });
+  }
+
+  /* Phase 6 REPAIR (Codex Critic BLOCK): window.uploadedMedia[src].url is an object URL --
+   * URL.createObjectURL(file) -- valid only inside THIS TAB'S SESSION. renderEl() binds it onto the
+   * live `.media` element for on-stage preview, which is fine there because the tab stays open; but
+   * exportElementHTML() below clones that live node, and a blob: URL baked into a downloaded HTML
+   * file dies the moment this tab closes (or immediately in any OTHER browser/tab), breaking every
+   * image/video in a file whose whole point is to keep working offline and standalone. Converts the
+   * SAME File object the PPTX export already multipart-uploads (window.uploadedMedia[src].file) to
+   * a data: URL instead, so the bytes travel inside the .html file itself. Cached on the entry so
+   * exporting twice (or two elements sharing one `src`) reads the File once.
+   */
+  function mediaDataURL(src) {
+    var entry = window.uploadedMedia && window.uploadedMedia[src];
+    if (!entry) return Promise.resolve('');
+    if (entry.dataUrl) return Promise.resolve(entry.dataUrl);
+    return fileToDataURL(entry.file).then(function (url) {
+      entry.dataUrl = url;
+      return url;
+    });
+  }
+
+  /* Phase 6 REPAIR (Codex Critic BLOCK): an ALLOWLIST, not a denylist. `javascript:`/`data:`/
+   * `vbscript:` etc. are refused by virtue of not being http/https/mailto -- not by name-matching
+   * every dangerous scheme there is, which is a list that only ever grows. The scheme regex below
+   * requires the whole run of characters up to the first ':' to be letters/digits/+/./- (RFC 3986
+   * scheme syntax) -- a control character or a space ANYWHERE in that run (e.g. a tab smuggled into
+   * "java\tscript:", which some browsers strip before reading the scheme) breaks the match and
+   * falls through to the "not one of the allowed forms" rejection below, so a browser-side strip-
+   * then-reinterpret trick cannot turn a rejected string into an accepted one. Returns '' (== "no
+   * link") for anything that is not http:, https:, mailto:, or an in-page anchor ('#...') --
+   * including a bare scheme-less string like "example.com", which is not one of the allowed forms
+   * either.
+   */
+  function sanitizeLink(link) {
+    var v = String(link || '').trim();
+    if (!v) return '';
+    if (v.charAt(0) === '#') return v;
+    var m = /^([a-zA-Z][a-zA-Z0-9+.-]*):/.exec(v);
+    if (!m) return '';
+    var scheme = m[1].toLowerCase();
+    return (scheme === 'http' || scheme === 'https' || scheme === 'mailto') ? v : '';
+  }
+
+  /* Phase 6 REPAIR #2 (Codex Critic BLOCK): `.txt .body` is `contenteditable`, and pasted rich text
+   * can carry markup this app never authored ??a nested `<a href="javascript:...">`, an
+   * `onerror`/`onload` attribute a browser did not strip on paste, a `style` with a legacy
+   * `expression()`/`-moz-binding` payload, and so on. The first repair cloned the live node and
+   * serialized it with `outerHTML`, which shipped all of that straight into a file this app's own
+   * caller then opens in a real browser ??exactly the reviewer's "bypasses sanitizeLink()" report,
+   * except the injection lands in the TEXT, not the link.
+   *
+   * Rather than trying to enumerate every unsafe tag/attribute (a denylist that only ever grows ??+   * the same reasoning sanitizeLink() above is built on), this reconstructs the body from the SAME
+   * normalizeText()/paragraphsToHTML() pair toIR() and loadIR() already use to move text through the
+   * IR (loadIR calls paragraphsToHTML() at roughly line 2693 above; see its own comment).
+   * normalizeText() reduces the live DOM to plain strings plus three formatting fields (bold,
+   * italic, color) and discards every tag/attribute in the process; paragraphsToHTML() rebuilds
+   * markup from ONLY that data, through esc() and a hard-coded `<div>/<span style="color:...">/
+   * <b>/<i>` shape. There is no path from a pasted `<script>` or an `onerror=` attribute to the
+   * reconstructed output, because neither one is a field that data shape carries ??the same
+   * "extract to a narrow trusted shape, then rebuild" strategy titleString() gives the title field
+   * below, applied to per-run formatting instead of a flat string.
+   */
+  function exportElementHTML(el, dataUrls) {
+    var clone = el.cloneNode(true);
+    stripEditorChrome(clone);
+    var type = el.dataset.type;
+    if (isMediaType(type)) {
+      var media = clone.querySelector('.media');
+      if (media) media.setAttribute('src', dataUrls[el.dataset.src] || '');
+      return clone.outerHTML;
+    }
+    var s = read(el);
+    var cloneBody = clone.querySelector('.txt .body');
+    if (cloneBody) {
+      var liveBody = el.querySelector('.txt .body');
+      var paragraphs = liveBody ? normalizeText(liveBody, s.size, s.color, s.bold, s.italic) : null;
+      cloneBody.innerHTML = paragraphs ? paragraphsToHTML(paragraphs, s.color) : '';
+      cloneBody.removeAttribute('contenteditable');
+    }
+    var link = sanitizeLink(s.link);
+    return link
+      ? '<a href="' + esc(link) + '" style="display:contents;cursor:pointer">' + clone.outerHTML + '</a>'
+      : clone.outerHTML;
+  }
+
+  async function exportHTML() {
+    clearWarnings();
+    var elements = Array.prototype.slice.call(stage.querySelectorAll(':scope > .el'));
+
+    // Same missing-media guard $('b-export') already runs before the PPTX round trip: a reload
+    // clears window.uploadedMedia, so a src an on-stage element still names may have no File behind
+    // it any more.
+    var srcs = [];
+    elements.forEach(function (el) {
+      if (isMediaType(el.dataset.type) && srcs.indexOf(el.dataset.src) < 0) srcs.push(el.dataset.src);
+    });
+    var missing = srcs.filter(function (n) { return !window.uploadedMedia || !window.uploadedMedia[n]; });
+    if (missing.length) {
+      warn(
+        'Cannot export ??missing media file(s): ' + missing.join(', '),
+        'This element references an image/video this browser tab no longer has the bytes for ' +
+        '(a reload clears them). Re-add it with the Media buttons in the toolbar, or delete the ' +
+        'element, then export again.'
+      );
+      setStatus('Export failed.');
+      return;
+    }
+
+    setStatus('Exporting??);
+
+    var dataUrls;
+    try {
+      dataUrls = {};
+      for (var i = 0; i < srcs.length; i++) dataUrls[srcs[i]] = await mediaDataURL(srcs[i]);
+    } catch (e) {
+      warn('Could not embed a media file', String(e));
+      setStatus('Export failed.');
+      return;
+    }
+
+    // Named so the export still succeeds when a link is unsafe -- the ELEMENT is not dropped, only
+    // its href -- but the user is told why the exported file's link does not work, rather than
+    // silently shipping a slide that looks clickable and is not.
+    var blockedLinks = elements
+      .filter(function (el) { var link = read(el).link; return link && !sanitizeLink(link); })
+      .map(function (el) { return el.dataset.id; });
+    if (blockedLinks.length) {
+      warn(
+        'Some links were not exported: ' + blockedLinks.join(', '),
+        'Only http:, https:, mailto: links and in-page anchors (#...) are allowed in exported HTML.'
+      );
+    }
+
+    var body = elements.map(function (el) { return exportElementHTML(el, dataUrls); }).join('\n');
+    // Phase 6 REPAIR #2 (Codex Critic BLOCK): titleField is ALSO a live contenteditable, with the
+    // same rich-paste risk exportElementHTML() defends the shape/textbox body against above.
+    // titleString() (used by serialize() for the very same field) already reduces it to a plain
+    // string plus '\n' for line breaks, discarding every tag and attribute on the way -- the title
+    // has no bold/italic/color of its own in the IR at all (schema.json's `title` is a bare string;
+    // see its description) -- so reconstructing through esc() here carries the identical guarantee
+    // exportElementHTML() relies on: nothing pasted into the title can become a tag or an attribute
+    // in the exported file.
+    var titleHTML = esc(titleString()).replace(/\n/g, '<br>');
+    var html = '<!DOCTYPE html>\n<html lang="ko">\n<head>\n<meta charset="utf-8">\n' +
+      '<meta name="viewport" content="width=device-width, initial-scale=1">\n' +
+      '<title>' + esc(safeStem()) + '</title>\n<style>' + EXPORT_CSS + '</style>\n</head>\n<body>\n' +
+      '<div id="stage">\n<div id="title-field">' + titleHTML + '</div>\n' +
+      body + '\n</div>\n</body>\n</html>\n';
+    download(new Blob([html], { type: 'text/html' }), safeStem() + '.html');
+    setStatus('Downloaded HTML (' + elements.length + ' element(s)).');
+  }
+
+  $('b-export-html').addEventListener('click', exportHTML);
+
   /* Load JSON ??a stretch goal, and the other half of the Gate 4 round trip. */
   $('b-load').addEventListener('click', function () { $('f-load').click(); });

+  /* Phase 6 REPAIR: a File -> its `data:<mime>;base64,...` URL, unlike fileToBase64() below (which
+   * strips that prefix for the server's pure-base64 field) -- an exported <img>/<video> `src` wants

+  function fileToDataURL(file) {

+   * URL.createObjectURL(file) -- valid only inside THIS TAB'S SESSION. renderEl() binds it onto the
+   * live `.media` element for on-stage preview, which is fine there because the tab stays open; but
+   * exportElementHTML() below clones that live node, and a blob: URL baked into a downloaded HTML
+   * file dies the moment this tab closes (or immediately in any OTHER browser/tab), breaking every
+   * image/video in a file whose whole point is to keep working offline and standalone. Converts the
+   * SAME File object the PPTX export already multipart-uploads (window.uploadedMedia[src].file) to
+   * a data: URL instead, so the bytes travel inside the .html file itself. Cached on the entry so
+   * exporting twice (or two elements sharing one `src`) reads the File once.
+   */
+  function mediaDataURL(src) {
+    var entry = window.uploadedMedia && window.uploadedMedia[src];
+    if (!entry) return Promise.resolve('');
+    if (entry.dataUrl) return Promise.resolve(entry.dataUrl);
+    return fileToDataURL(entry.file).then(function (url) {
+      entry.dataUrl = url;
+      return url;
+    });
+  }
+
+  /* Phase 6 REPAIR (Codex Critic BLOCK): an ALLOWLIST, not a denylist. `javascript:`/`data:`/
+   * `vbscript:` etc. are refused by virtue of not being http/https/mailto -- not by name-matching
+   * every dangerous scheme there is, which is a list that only ever grows. The scheme regex below
+   * requires the whole run of characters up to the first ':' to be letters/digits/+/./- (RFC 3986
+   * scheme syntax) -- a control character or a space ANYWHERE in that run (e.g. a tab smuggled into
+   * "java\tscript:", which some browsers strip before reading the scheme) breaks the match and
+   * falls through to the "not one of the allowed forms" rejection below, so a browser-side strip-
+   * then-reinterpret trick cannot turn a rejected string into an accepted one. Returns '' (== "no
+   * link") for anything that is not http:, https:, mailto:, or an in-page anchor ('#...') --
+   * including a bare scheme-less string like "example.com", which is not one of the allowed forms
+   * either.
+   */
+  function sanitizeLink(link) {
+    var v = String(link || '').trim();
+    if (!v) return '';
+    if (v.charAt(0) === '#') return v;
+    var m = /^([a-zA-Z][a-zA-Z0-9+.-]*):/.exec(v);
+    if (!m) return '';
+    var scheme = m[1].toLowerCase();
+    return (scheme === 'http' || scheme === 'https' || scheme === 'mailto') ? v : '';
+  }
+
+  /* Phase 6 REPAIR #2 (Codex Critic BLOCK): `.txt .body` is `contenteditable`, and pasted rich text
+   * can carry markup this app never authored — a nested `<a href="javascript:...">`, an
+   * `onerror`/`onload` attribute a browser did not strip on paste, a `style` with a legacy
+   * `expression()`/`-moz-binding` payload, and so on. The first repair cloned the live node and
+   * serialized it with `outerHTML`, which shipped all of that straight into a file this app's own
+   * caller then opens in a real browser — exactly the reviewer's "bypasses sanitizeLink()" report,
+   * except the injection lands in the TEXT, not the link.
+   *
+   * Rather than trying to enumerate every unsafe tag/attribute (a denylist that only ever grows —
+   * the same reasoning sanitizeLink() above is built on), this reconstructs the body from the SAME
+   * normalizeText()/paragraphsToHTML() pair toIR() and loadIR() already use to move text through the
+   * IR (loadIR calls paragraphsToHTML() at roughly line 2693 above; see its own comment).
+   * normalizeText() reduces the live DOM to plain strings plus three formatting fields (bold,
+   * italic, color) and discards every tag/attribute in the process; paragraphsToHTML() rebuilds
+   * markup from ONLY that data, through esc() and a hard-coded `<div>/<span style="color:...">/
+   * <b>/<i>` shape. There is no path from a pasted `<script>` or an `onerror=` attribute to the
+   * reconstructed output, because neither one is a field that data shape carries — the same
+   * "extract to a narrow trusted shape, then rebuild" strategy titleString() gives the title field
+   * below, applied to per-run formatting instead of a flat string.
+   */
+  function exportElementHTML(el, dataUrls) {
+    var clone = el.cloneNode(true);
+    stripEditorChrome(clone);
+    var type = el.dataset.type;
+    if (isMediaType(type)) {
+      var media = clone.querySelector('.media');
+      if (media) media.setAttribute('src', dataUrls[el.dataset.src] || '');
+      return clone.outerHTML;
+    }
+    var s = read(el);
+    var cloneBody = clone.querySelector('.txt .body');
+    if (cloneBody) {
+      var liveBody = el.querySelector('.txt .body');
+      var paragraphs = liveBody ? normalizeText(liveBody, s.size, s.color, s.bold, s.italic) : null;
+      cloneBody.innerHTML = paragraphs ? paragraphsToHTML(paragraphs, s.color) : '';
+      cloneBody.removeAttribute('contenteditable');
+    }
+    var link = sanitizeLink(s.link);
+    return link
+      ? '<a href="' + esc(link) + '" style="display:contents;cursor:pointer">' + clone.outerHTML + '</a>'
+      : clone.outerHTML;
+  }
+
+  async function exportHTML() {
+    clearWarnings();
+    var elements = Array.prototype.slice.call(stage.querySelectorAll(':scope > .el'));
+
+    // Same missing-media guard $('b-export') already runs before the PPTX round trip: a reload
+    // clears window.uploadedMedia, so a src an on-stage element still names may have no File behind
+    // it any more.
+    var srcs = [];
+    elements.forEach(function (el) {
+      if (isMediaType(el.dataset.type) && srcs.indexOf(el.dataset.src) < 0) srcs.push(el.dataset.src);
+    });
+    var missing = srcs.filter(function (n) { return !window.uploadedMedia || !window.uploadedMedia[n]; });
+    if (missing.length) {
+      warn(
+        'Cannot export — missing media file(s): ' + missing.join(', '),
+        'This element references an image/video this browser tab no longer has the bytes for ' +
+        '(a reload clears them). Re-add it with the Media buttons in the toolbar, or delete the ' +
+        'element, then export again.'
+      );
+      setStatus('Export failed.');
+      return;
+    }
+
+    setStatus('Exporting…');
+
+    var dataUrls;
+    try {
+      dataUrls = {};
+      for (var i = 0; i < srcs.length; i++) dataUrls[srcs[i]] = await mediaDataURL(srcs[i]);
+    } catch (e) {
+      warn('Could not embed a media file', String(e));
+      setStatus('Export failed.');
+      return;
+    }
+
+    // Named so the export still succeeds when a link is unsafe -- the ELEMENT is not dropped, only
+    // its href -- but the user is told why the exported file's link does not work, rather than
+    // silently shipping a slide that looks clickable and is not.
+    var blockedLinks = elements
+      .filter(function (el) { var link = read(el).link; return link && !sanitizeLink(link); })
+      .map(function (el) { return el.dataset.id; });
+    if (blockedLinks.length) {
+      warn(
+        'Some links were not exported: ' + blockedLinks.join(', '),
+        'Only http:, https:, mailto: links and in-page anchors (#...) are allowed in exported HTML.'
+      );
+    }
+
+    var body = elements.map(function (el) { return exportElementHTML(el, dataUrls); }).join('\n');
+    // Phase 6 REPAIR #2 (Codex Critic BLOCK): titleField is ALSO a live contenteditable, with the
+    // same rich-paste risk exportElementHTML() defends the shape/textbox body against above.
+    // titleString() (used by serialize() for the very same field) already reduces it to a plain
+    // string plus '\n' for line breaks, discarding every tag and attribute on the way -- the title
+    // has no bold/italic/color of its own in the IR at all (schema.json's `title` is a bare string;
+    // see its description) -- so reconstructing through esc() here carries the identical guarantee
+    // exportElementHTML() relies on: nothing pasted into the title can become a tag or an attribute
+    // in the exported file.
+    var titleHTML = esc(titleString()).replace(/\n/g, '<br>');
+    var html = '<!DOCTYPE html>\n<html lang="ko">\n<head>\n<meta charset="utf-8">\n' +
+      '<meta name="viewport" content="width=device-width, initial-scale=1">\n' +
+      '<title>' + esc(safeStem()) + '</title>\n<style>' + EXPORT_CSS + '</style>\n</head>\n<body>\n' +
+      '<div id="stage">\n<div id="title-field">' + titleHTML + '</div>\n' +
+      body + '\n</div>\n</body>\n</html>\n';
+    download(new Blob([html], { type: 'text/html' }), safeStem() + '.html');
+    setStatus('Downloaded HTML (' + elements.length + ' element(s)).');
+  }
+
+  $('b-export-html').addEventListener('click', exportHTML);
+
   /* Load JSON — a stretch goal, and the other half of the Gate 4 round trip. */
   $('b-load').addEventListener('click', function () { $('f-load').click(); });
 
@@ -2618,6 +2848,9 @@

# Phase 6 Frontend: Advanced Media & Editor Features

## CRITICAL INSTRUCTION
You MUST apply your edits to `.editwork/tasks/AI_Powered_PPT/src/aippt/canvas/static/...`.

## Tasks

### 1. Media Files (Image/Video) Support
- **UI**: Add "Add Image" and "Add Video" buttons to the sidebar toolbar. When clicked, trigger a file input (`<input type="file" accept="image/*">` etc.).
- **State**: When a user selects a file, read the file and store the `File` object in a global map (e.g., `window.uploadedMedia = window.uploadedMedia || {}; uploadedMedia[file.name] = file;`). 
- **DOM**: Add a new element to the `ir.slides[activeSlideIndex].elements` array with `type: "image"` or `"video"`, and `src: file.name`. Render an `<img>` or `<video>` tag inside the stage so it can be resized/moved like text boxes. (Use `URL.createObjectURL(file)` as the DOM `src`).
- **Export**: Modify `exportPPT()` in `editor.js`. Change the fetch call to `/api/download`. Instead of sending JSON, use `FormData`. `formData.append("ir", JSON.stringify(payload))`. Then loop through `uploadedMedia` and `formData.append(filename, fileObj)`. Remove `application/json` headers so the browser sets the multipart boundary automatically.

### 2. Multi-selection & Alignment
- **Selection**: Allow Shift+Click in `editor.js` / `interactions.js` to select multiple elements. Keep track of them (e.g., `window.selectedElementIds = [...]`).
- **UI**: Add Alignment buttons in the properties panel: Align Top, Bottom, Left, Right, Center X, Center Y.
- **Logic**: When alignment buttons are clicked, find all selected elements, determine the bounding box (e.g. min Y for Top align), and update their `x` or `y` values.

### 3. Copy / Paste
- Add global keyboard listeners for `Ctrl+C` (or `Cmd+C`) and `Ctrl+V` (or `Cmd+V`).
- On Copy, serialize the currently selected element(s) into memory.
- On Paste, duplicate them with a new `id`, slightly offset (`x + 20`, `y + 20`), and add to the current slide.

### 4. Transparency (Alpha) & 1pt Border
- **Alpha**: Add an opacity slider (0-100%) in the style panel for Fill Color. When generating the color string for the IR, if opacity is < 100%, append the hex alpha value to the color (e.g. `#FF000080` for 50% opacity red). Update the DOM element's style to reflect this (`rgba` or `opacity`).
- **Border**: Add a "1pt Border" toggle (checkbox) in the properties panel. If checked, add `border: { color: "#000000", width: 1 }` to the IR element, and show a `1px solid black` border on the DOM element.

### 5. FIX THE MULTI-SELECTION STATE LEAK (Bug Fix)
- You previously implemented multi-selection, but there was a state leak. `var multi` (the alignment set) was not cleared during `restore()` (undo/redo) or `loadIR()`.
- **Action Required**: Add a `clearMulti()` function and call it inside `restore()` (before the new snapshot is applied) and inside `loadIR()` (when clearing the stage) to ensure no detached DOM elements remain in the `multi` array.
- Also ensure that deleting the `selected` element removes it from `multi`.

I've completed the repair. Summary:

**What was wrong:** The blocked diff's `exportElementHTML()` serialized the live `contenteditable` `.txt .body` via `clone.outerHTML`, and `exportHTML()` used `titleField.innerHTML` directly — both would carry through unsafe markup (event-handler attributes, `javascript:` links, etc.) from a rich paste into the exported standalone `.html` file, executing on open.

**Fix applied** (in the staged worktree `stg.ZQ9vgXVn`, since it started at the pre-Phase-6-HTML-export baseline — this was rebuilt whole, not patched):
- `exportElementHTML()` now reconstructs each shape/textbox body via `normalizeText()` + `paragraphsToHTML()` — the same normalize/rebuild pair `toIR()` and `loadIR()` already use — which discards every tag/attribute and keeps only `{text, bold, italic, color}`, then re-emits a fixed safe `<div>/<span>/<b>/<i>` shape through `esc()`.
- `exportHTML()` now builds the title from `esc(titleString()).replace(/\n/g, '<br>')` instead of `titleField.innerHTML`, since `titleString()` already reduces the title to a plain string.
- The previously-accepted fixes (blob→`data:` URL media embedding, `sanitizeLink()` scheme allowlist) were carried forward unchanged.

I also hit the same `write_scope`-prefix bug documented in memory (temp brief's `write_scope` was missing the `tasks/AI_Powered_PPT/` prefix for this repo-rooted stage) — edited the real files and documented the mismatch rather than the literal (nonexistent) path.

  655:   function shapeMarkup(s) {

  657:     // Preview-only: SVG's fill-opacity is how this stage shows the alpha byte toIR() will fold

 1228:   function cssColorToHex(raw) {
 1229:     var v = String(raw).trim();
 1230:     var m = v.match(/^rgba?\(\s*(\d+)\s*,\s*(\d+)\s*,\s*(\d+)/i);
 1231:     if (m) return '#' + hex2(+m[1]) + hex2(+m[2]) + hex2(+m[3]);
 1232:     m = v.match(/^#([0-9a-f]{6})$/i);
 1233:     if (m) return '#' + m[1].toUpperCase();
 1234:     m = v.match(/^#([0-9a-f]{3})$/i);
 1235:     if (m) {
 1236:       var s = m[1];
 1237:       return '#' + (s[0] + s[0] + s[1] + s[1] + s[2] + s[2]).toUpperCase();
 1238:     }
 1239:     return null; // a named colour or anything exotic: fall back to the element default

 1254:   /* normalizeText(root) -> [paragraph, ...] | null
 1255:    *
 1256:    * Every run carries t, size and color -- all three are REQUIRED by the schema. An un-coloured run
 1257:    * inherits fontRef -> lt1, which is WHITE, and that is the rule that stops white-on-white text.
 1258:    *
 1259:    * defBold/defItalic are the ELEMENT's flags, and they are OR-ed into every run -- the same
 1260:    * relationship defSize and defColor already have with the runs, just spelled out. The IR has no
 1261:    * element-level bold to serialize them into; run.bold is the only place they can land. A <b> or a
 1262:    * <strong> INSIDE the body (a paste, or the browser's own Ctrl+B) still bolds its own run on its
 1263:    * own, which is why turning the element flag off has to remove those tags -- see setTextFlag().
 1264:    */
 1265:   function normalizeText(root, defSize, defColor, defBold, defItalic) {
 1266:     var paras = [];
 1267:     var cur = null;
 1268: 
 1269:     function startPara() { cur = []; paras.push(cur); }
 1270:     startPara();
 1271: 
 1272:     function push(text, fmt) {
 1273:       if (!text) return;
 1274:       var run = { t: text, size: defSize, color: fmt.color || defColor };
 1275:       if (fmt.bold || defBold) run.bold = true;
 1276:       if (fmt.italic || defItalic) run.italic = true;
 1277:       var last = cur[cur.length - 1];
 1278:       // Merge adjacent runs with identical formatting.
 1279:       if (last && !!last.bold === !!run.bold && !!last.italic === !!run.italic && last.color === run.color) {
 1280:         last.t += run.t;
 1281:       } else {
 1282:         cur.push(run);
 1283:       }
 1284:     }
 1285: 
 1286:     function walk(node, fmt) {
 1287:       var kids = node.childNodes;
 1288:       for (var i = 0; i < kids.length; i++) {
 1289:         var child = kids[i];
 1290: 
 1291:         if (child.nodeType === 3) { // text
 1292:           push(stripC0(child.nodeValue), fmt);
 1293:           continue;
 1294:         }
 1295:         if (child.nodeType !== 1) continue; // comments and the rest
 1296: 
 1297:         var tag = child.tagName;
 1298: 
 1299:         if (tag === 'BR') {
 1300:           // A <br> that is the LAST child of its block is the browser's own filler -- Chrome puts
 1301:           // one in every empty contenteditable block to give it height. Emitting it would add a
 1302:           // phantom trailing line to the PPTX.
 1303:           if (child === node.lastChild) continue;
 1304:           push('\n', fmt); // style.py splits on \n and emits <a:br/>
 1305:           continue;
 1306:         }
 1307: 
 1308:         var f = { bold: fmt.bold, italic: fmt.italic, color: fmt.color };
 1309:         if (tag === 'B' || tag === 'STRONG') f.bold = true;
 1310:         if (tag === 'I' || tag === 'EM') f.italic = true;
 1311:         var c = colorOf(child);
 1312:         if (c) f.color = c;
 1313: 
 1314:         if (BLOCK_TAGS[tag]) startPara(); // a block element is a NEW PARAGRAPH
 1315:         walk(child, f);
 1316:       }
 1317:     }
 1318: 
 1319:     walk(root, { bold: false, italic: false, color: null });
 1320: 
 1321:     // Drop empty runs, then empty paragraphs -- browsers leave trailing empty <div>s behind
 1322:     // constantly.
 1323:     var out = [];
 1324:     for (var p = 0; p < paras.length; p++) {
 1325:       var runs = paras[p].filter(function (r) { return r.t !== ''; });
 1326:       if (runs.length) out.push({ runs: runs });
 1327:     }
 1328:     // If nothing survives, the caller emits "text": null -- NOT paragraphs: [], which the schema
 1329:     // rejects (minItems: 1).
 1330:     return out.length ? out : null;
 1331:   }
 1332: 
 1333:   // The title is the same walk, flattened to a plain STRING. A "\n" becomes an <a:br/> in
 1334:   // placeholder 10.
 1335:   function titleString() {
 1336:     var out = '';
 1337:     (function walk(node) {
 1338:       var kids = node.childNodes;
 1339:       for (var i = 0; i < kids.length; i++) {
 1340:         var child = kids[i];
 1341:         if (child.nodeType === 3) { out += child.nodeValue; continue; }
 1342:         if (child.nodeType !== 1) continue;
 1343:         if (child.tagName === 'BR') {
 1344:           if (child !== node.lastChild) out += '\n';
 1345:           continue;
 1346:         }
 1347:         if (BLOCK_TAGS[child.tagName] && out !== '') out += '\n';
 1348:         walk(child);
 1349:       }

 1351:     return stripC0(out).replace(/\n+$/, '');
 1352:   }

 1356:   // Folds a 0-100 opacity PERCENT into the trailing AA byte of a #RRGGBB colour, per schema.json's
 1357:   // `color` def (#RRGGBB, or #RRGGBBAA where the trailing byte is alpha). 100 (opaque) emits no
 1358:   // byte at all -- an unmodified 6-digit colour is the common case and stays exactly what it was
 1359:   // before Phase 6, so decks with no transparency are byte-identical to what this editor emitted
 1360:   // before this feature existed.
 1361:   function withAlpha(hex6, alphaPct) {

 2746:   /* An IR element -> editor state.
 2747:    *
 2748:    * It NEVER throws and it NEVER repairs, and both halves of that are load-bearing.
 2749:    *
 2750:    * Not throwing is what lets the gate judge the WHOLE file and report every bad element at once,
 2751:    * instead of dying on the first one. `text`, `paragraphs`, `runs` and the first run are each
 2752:    * optional AND each hand-editable, so `text.paragraphs[0].runs[0]` -- which is what this used to
 2753:    * be -- is a TypeError in any file someone deleted a line from.
 2754:    *
 2755:    * Not repairing is what keeps the judge honest: a missing coordinate must arrive at
 2756:    * illegalReason() still missing, so that it is REFUSED, rather than quietly defaulted to 0 and
 2757:    * drawn somewhere the user never put it.
 2758:    */
 2759:   function stateFromIR(e) {

 2794:     /* The element's bold/italic flags are a SUMMARY of the runs: on only when EVERY run is bold.
 2795:      *
 2796:      * Reading them off the FIRST run -- the way size and color are read -- would be wrong here, and
 2797:      * destructively so. The flag is OR-ed into every run on the way back out (normalizeText), so
 2798:      * "the first run is bold" would mean a deck with one bold word in a plain sentence reloads with
 2799:      * the flag on, and re-exports with the WHOLE sentence bold. A round trip must not change the
 2800:      * deck. Mixed runs therefore summarise to OFF, and their own <b>/<i> tags -- which
 2801:      * paragraphsToHTML() writes -- carry the per-run bold instead. Nothing is lost either way.
 2802:      *
 2803:      * Inlined rather than factored into a helper on purpose: this function is lifted out by name and
 2804:      * run in Node by tests/canvas/test_app.py, and a call to a helper that the harness does not also
 2805:      * lift is a ReferenceError in the gate rather than a failure in the code.

 2820:     // withAlpha() in toIR()) -- inlined rather than a shared helper for the same reason the
 2821:     // bold/italic summary above is: stateFromIR() is lifted by name into a harness that does not
 2822:     // also lift withAlpha()/hex2(), and a call out to either would be a ReferenceError there, not a

 2857:   /* THE SECOND CALLER OF THE JUDGE, and the reason the judge exists as its own function.
 2858:    *
 2859:    * ALL OR NOTHING, and in this order: judge every element FIRST, touch the stage SECOND.
 2860:    *
 2861:    * A file is not a trusted input just because this editor may have written it -- it is a
 2862:    * hand-editable text file, and the moment one of its elements could come to rest on the logo,
 2863:    * "the reserved regions are strictly enforced" stopped being a true sentence. They were enforced
 2864:    * against the mouse and not against a file.
 2865:    *
 2866:    * The ORDER is the other half of the fix, and it is not cosmetic. This function used to clear the
 2867:    * stage and overwrite the title before it had looked at a single element, so a per-element check
 2868:    * bolted into the loop -- however correct that check was -- could only ever leave a half-imported
 2869:    * stage behind and the user's own deck already gone. "Reject the entire import" is not reachable
 2870:    * from inside that loop. It is reachable from here.

 2872:   function loadIR(ir) {
 2873:     if (!ir || typeof ir !== 'object') throw new Error('that file does not contain a deck');
 2874:     if (ir.schema !== SCHEMA) throw new Error('schema is ' + ir.schema + ', expected ' + SCHEMA);
 2875:     if (!ir.canvas || ir.canvas.w !== CANVAS.w || ir.canvas.h !== CANVAS.h) {

 2892:                '", type "' + (typeof s.type === 'string' ? s.type : '?') + '")';

  440:   /* Centre origin, degrees CLOCKWISE -- identical to shape.rotation in python-pptx and to
  441:      DrawingML a:xfrm/@rot. No conversion anywhere. */
  442:   transform: rotate(var(--rot, 0deg));
  443:   transform-origin: 50% 50%;
  444: }
  445: 
  446: /* Editor-only affordances use OUTLINE, never BORDER. A border participates in the box model and
  447:    would drift the element's geometry by border/2 -- a coordinate bug wearing a styling hat. */
  448: .el.selected { outline: 1px solid #1e88ff; outline-offset: 0; }
  449: 
  450: /* Reject-and-hold: an illegal frame is not committed, and the element flashes instead of moving. */
  451: .el.blocked { outline: 2px solid #FF0032; animation: blocked .18s ease-out 2; }
  452: @keyframes blocked { 50% { outline-color: transparent; } }
  453: 
  454: /* The handles come AFTER the <svg> and the .txt in DOM order, and positioned siblings paint in DOM
  455:    order, so they land on top without a z-index. That is not a coincidence being relied on -- it is
  456:    the same rule the elements themselves use. NOTHING INSIDE #stage CARRIES A z-index, on purpose:
  457:    z-order is DOM order and nothing else, and a second source of truth would desync. (The only
  458:    z-index in this file is on #warnings, which is app chrome outside the stage and is never
  459:    serialized.) */
  460: .el .handle {
  461:   position: absolute;
  462:   display: none;                       /* only the selected element wears handles */
  463:   width: 8px;
  464:   height: 8px;
  465:   background: #fff;
  466:   outline: 1px solid #1e88ff;
  467: }
  468: .el.selected .handle { display: block; }
  469: .el .handle[data-h='nw'] { left: -4px;              top: -4px;               cursor: nwse-resize; }
  470: .el .handle[data-h='n']  { left: calc(50% - 4px);   top: -4px;               cursor: ns-resize;   }
  471: .el .handle[data-h='ne'] { right: -4px;             top: -4px;               cursor: nesw-resize; }
  472: .el .handle[data-h='e']  { right: -4px;             top: calc(50% - 4px);    cursor: ew-resize;   }
  473: .el .handle[data-h='se'] { right: -4px;             bottom: -4px;            cursor: nwse-resize; }
  474: .el .handle[data-h='s']  { left: calc(50% - 4px);   bottom: -4px;            cursor: ns-resize;   }
  475: .el .handle[data-h='sw'] { left: -4px;              bottom: -4px;            cursor: nesw-resize; }
  476: .el .handle[data-h='w']  { left: -4px;              top: calc(50% - 4px);    cursor: ew-resize;   }
  477: 
  478: .el .handle[data-h='rot'] {
  479:   left: calc(50% - 4px);
  480:   top: -22px;
  481:   border-radius: 50%;
  482:   cursor: grab;
  483: }
  484: /* The endpoint handles of a connector. */
  485: .el .handle[data-h='p1'], .el .handle[data-h='p2'] { border-radius: 50%; cursor: move; }
  486: 
  487: /* [REDACTED] CONTRACT: the text box (5.6) VERBATIM */
  488: 
  489: .el .txt {
  490:   position: absolute;
  491:   /* PowerPoint's default text-frame insets: 0.1in L/R, 0.05in T/B = 7.2 / 3.6 logical units.
  492:      Hard-coded on both sides. This is the trap that reads like a coordinate bug. */
  493:   left: 7.2px; right: 7.2px; top: 3.6px; bottom: 3.6px;
  494:   box-sizing: border-box;
  495:   padding: 0; margin: 0; border: 0;
  496: 
  497:   font-family: 'Century Gothic', 'Malgun Gothic', sans-serif;  /* mirrors latin/ea split */
  498:   font-size: 14px;              /* == 14 pt in the model */
  499:   line-height: 1.2;             /* must equal renderer line_spacing */
  500:   white-space: pre-wrap;        /* word_wrap = True */
  501:   overflow-wrap: break-word;
  502:   word-break: keep-all;         /* Korean: break at spaces, not mid-word */
  503: 
  504:   display: flex;                /* autoshape default is anchor="ctr" + algn="ctr" */
  505:   align-items: center;          /* valign ctr  -> tf.vertical_anchor = MSO_ANCHOR.MIDDLE */
  506:   justify-content: center;      /* align  ctr  -> p.alignment = PP_ALIGN.CENTER */
  507:   text-align: center;
  508:   overflow: visible;            /* show overflow; do NOT hide the problem */
  509: 
  510:   outline: none;
  511: 
  512:   /* The text box covers almost the whole element, so if it swallowed pointer events the user could
  513:      never DRAG a textbox -- every grab would land a caret instead. Click selects and drags;
  514:      DOUBLE-click opens the text for editing (editor.js adds .editing). This is the conventional
  515:      bargain and it is the only one that leaves a no-fill textbox movable at all. */
  516:   pointer-events: none;
  517: }
  518: .el.editing .txt { pointer-events: auto; cursor: text; }
  519: 
  520: /* CONTRACT: run.bold / run.italic.
  521:  *
  522:  *     .el[data-bold='1']   <->  style.py: run.font.bold   = True   (every run in the element)
  523:  *     .el[data-italic='1'] <->  style.py: run.font.italic = True
  524:  *
  525:  * These are ELEMENT-level flags over a PER-RUN property: normalizeText() OR-s them into every run
  526:  * on the way out, because run.bold is the only place the IR can carry them. A <b> inside .body still
  527:  * bolds its own run by itself -- so these rules are the preview of the FLAG, not of the tags, and
  528:  * the two are additive exactly as they are in the serializer.
  529:  *
  530:  * The value is the string '1'. dataset writes strings, so a false flag is '' -- never 'false',
  531:  * which would be truthy on the way back in and match nothing on the way down here. */
  532: .el[data-bold='1'] .txt   { font-weight: 700; }
  533: .el[data-italic='1'] .txt { font-style: italic; }
  534: 
  535: /* valign / align are written from state onto these data-* hooks, never read back out of CSS. */
  536: .el .txt[data-valign='TOP']    { align-items: flex-start; }
  537: .el .txt[data-valign='MIDDLE'] { align-items: center; }
  538: .el .txt[data-valign='BOTTOM'] { align-items: flex-end; }
  539: .el .txt[data-align='LEFT']    { justify-content: flex-start; text-align: left; }
  540: .el .txt[data-align='CENTER']  { justify-content: center;     text-align: center; }
  541: .el .txt[data-align='RIGHT']   { justify-content: flex-end;   text-align: right; }
  542: 
  543: /* THE single flex item. The Sec 5.6 contract above puts `display: flex` on .txt, and its
  544:    align-items/justify-content mapping (valign/align) is only correct when there is exactly ONE
  545:    flex item. A contenteditable that holds several paragraphs produces several block children --
  546:    which, as direct flex items, would lay out SIDE BY SIDE in a row. So .txt is the anchor box and
  547:    .body is the contenteditable: one flex item, paragraphs stacking normally inside it, and the
  548:    contract preserved exactly as written. */
  549: .el .txt > .body { width: 100%; outline: none; }
  550: 
  551: .el svg { overflow: visible; position: absolute; inset: 0; }
  552: 
  553: /* A connector is a line: it has no text frame and its SVG must be free to draw its stroke and
  554:    arrowheads outside the endpoints' AABB. */
  555: .el[data-type='connector'] { overflow: visible; }
  556: 
  557: /* [REDACTED] Phase 6: image / video elements */
  558: 
  559: /* Geometrically an image/video is a rect (position/size come from .el, same as everything else);
  560:    the tag itself just fills the box. object-fit: fill matches add_picture()/add_movie(), which
  561:    stretch to the given width/height with no letterboxing of their own. pointer-events stays AUTO
  562:    (unlike .txt) -- there is no separate overlay here for a click to fall through to, so the drag
  563:    handler on .el (which listens on .el itself and sees the bubbled event) needs nothing special. */
  564: .el .media { position: absolute; inset: 0; width: 100%; height: 100%; object-fit: fill; }

# Task Overview
We are adding an HTML Export feature and extending the `canvas-ir/1` schema to support hyperlinks (`link` property) so that exported HTML can have interactive links (e.g. to a separate algorithm explanation slide). 

## Requirements
1. **IR Schema Extension (`schema.json`)**:
   - Add a `"link": {"type": "string"}` property to the schema. The safest place is either at the element level (for shapes) or inside the text run. Given PPTX usually links shapes or text, adding it to the `shape` definition (or globally to all elements via `additionalProperties` or similar) is easiest. Let's add it to `shape` (rect, ellipse, triangle, textbox) as an optional `"link": {"type": "string"}`.

2. **Frontend UI (`index.html` & `editor.js`)**:
   - Add a `<label>Link <input type="url" id="p-link"></label>` to `aside#props` in `index.html`.
   - Update `editor.js` to read/write this `link` property from the element's dataset.
   - Add a "Download HTML" button in `index.html` (e.g. `<button type="button" id="b-export-html">Download HTML</button>`) next to the "Export PPT" button.

3. **HTML Export Logic (`editor.js`)**:
   - Implement the `exportHTML()` function tied to the new "Download HTML" button.
   - The export must bundle the `div#stage` contents into a standalone HTML string (including necessary CSS from `style.css` or inline styles for absolute positioning).
   - Elements that have a `link` property should be wrapped in an `<a>` tag or have an `onclick` event to navigate to the link when clicked in the exported HTML.
   - Trigger a browser download of `presentation.html`.

## Guidelines
- Follow the single-source-of-truth geometry rules in `editor.js`.
- Make sure the exported HTML visually matches the canvas (absolute positioning, correct colors, z-index).
- Adhere strictly to the write_scope. Do not touch backend python files unless necessary (they shouldn't be, as schema.json drives validation).

## Reviewer Feedback (BLOCK)
- `editor.js:2203-2215` and `editor.js:2269` serialize live `contenteditable` markup through `clone.outerHTML` and `titleField.innerHTML`. Rich pasted content can therefore preserve unsafe tags, event handlers, or nested `javascript:` links, bypassing `sanitizeLink()` and executing when the exported file opens. Reconstruct text from normalized/escaped data or sanitize cloned DOM with strict tag/attribute allowlists.

`clone.outerHTML`/`titleField.innerHTML`) and attaches `claude-main-diff-1989583-17592.txt` as "your

text is `[REDACTED].txt`, reviewing diff `claude-main-diff-1934939-13128.txt`
(11:11) — an EARLIER, still-vulnerable diff. The attached `1989583` diff (11:45) is actually the
`phase6_repair2` worker's OWN fix for that same feedback (its `[REDACTED].txt`
narrates exactly this fix, and the diff's own comments are literally tagged "Phase 6 REPAIR #2 (Codex
Critic BLOCK)"). So the attached diff was not itself rejected for contentEditable serialization — it
already contains the fix the brief asks for. This does not change what to do: the diff is the correct,
already-reviewed design, and the actual bug (per "Starting state" above) is that no staging worktree so
far has ever had it durably applied. Applying this diff's content to the real `write_scope` files is
exactly what round 4 needed to do regardless of which round's BLOCK originally prompted the design.

## What was applied

Applied `artifacts/claude-main-diff-1989583-17592.txt` verbatim to the write_scope files (all hunks
matched the pre-existing baseline exactly; no adaptation needed):

- `schema.json`: added `$defs.link` (`{"type": "string", "description": "..."}`) and
  `"link": {"$ref": "#/$defs/link"}` to the four shape branches (`rect`, `ellipse`, `triangle`,
  `textbox`), after their existing `"text"` property. NOT added to `connector`/`image`/`video`.
- `index.html`: added `#b-export-html` ("Download HTML") next to `#b-export` in the Export group, and a
  `data-for="shape"` "Link" section (`#p-link`, `type="url"`) in the property panel, right before the
  `data-for="connector"` Connector section.
- `editor.js`:
  - `DEFAULTS.shape` / `DEFAULTS.textbox` gained `link: ''`.
  - `read()` / `write()` gained the `d.link` <-> `s.link` round trip on the shared non-connector,
    non-media branch.
  - `toIR()` emits `out.link = s.link` only when truthy (same pattern as `out.stroke`).
  - `stateFromIR()` gained `link: typeof e.link === 'string' ? e.link : def.link`.
  - `p-link` added to the `txControl(...)` list and given an `input` listener guarded by
    `!isConnector() && !isMedia()`.
  - `syncProps()` populates `$('p-link').value = s.link || ''`.
  - New block (inserted immediately before the `/* Load JSON */` comment, after the `$('b-export')`
    PPTX handler): `EXPORT_CSS`, `stripEditorChrome()`, `fileToDataURL()`, `mediaDataURL()`,
    `sanitizeLink()`, `exportElementHTML()`, `exportHTML()`, and the `$('b-export-html')` click wiring.

**The security fix this round exists to (re-)deliver, specifically:** `exportElementHTML()` does NOT
serialize the live `contenteditable` `.txt .body` via `outerHTML`/`innerHTML`. It clones the live `.el`
for geometry/inline-style fidelity, but for a non-media element it re-derives `paragraphs` from the LIVE
body via `normalizeText(liveBody, s.size, s.color, s.bold, s.italic)` — the same function `toIR()` uses
for the JSON/PPTX export path, which discards every tag/attribute and keeps only `{text, bold, italic,
color}` — then overwrites the clone's body with `paragraphsToHTML(paragraphs, s.color)`, which only ever
emits a fixed `<div>/<span style="color:...">/<b>/<i>` shape through `esc()`. There is no data path from
a pasted `<script>`/`onerror=` attribute into the reconstructed output, because neither is a field that
shape carries. The exported title has the same treatment: `esc(titleString()).replace(/\n/g, '<br>')`
instead of `titleField.innerHTML` (`titleString()` is the same plain-text-plus-`\n` walk `serialize()`
already uses for the title). `sanitizeLink()` (the element `link` PROPERTY's http/https/mailto/`#anchor`
allowlist) and the blob→`data:` URL media-embedding fix (`fileToDataURL()`/`mediaDataURL()`) are both
carried forward unchanged, per the brief's explicit instruction.

## Verification performed (and what could not be run)

No interpreter/VCS command (`python -c`, `node --check`, `git status`) could be run in this session —
every `Bash` attempt came back "This command requires approval" with no prompt surfacing, consistent
with `[[[REDACTED]]]`. `Edit`/`Write`/`Bash mkdir`/`Grep`/`Read` all
worked unprompted against both the staging worktree and this outer-tree path (this session landed on the
permissive end of that memory's observed split). `PowerShell`'s `ConvertFrom-Json` cmdlet (not a
general-purpose interpreter invocation) ran unprompted and confirmed `schema.json` parses as valid JSON
after all four edits. A `[regex]::Matches(...)`-based brace-balance check was blocked by the PowerShell
tool's own sandbox ("Command contains subexpressions $()"), so no automated brace count was obtained for
`editor.js`; instead, every inserted/modified region was re-read via `Read` after editing and hand-
checked for matching braces/parens against the source diff, which is a byte-for-byte match (I verified
this file section against the diff character-for-character rather than re-deriving it), so it carries the
same syntactic correctness as whatever review already passed it on the JS-syntax dimension.

Verified by hand:
- Every one of the diff's 4 `schema.json` hunks (rect/ellipse/triangle/textbox) and the new `$defs.link`
  entry applied at the correct anchor, confirmed by `Grep` showing exactly 4 occurrences of
  `"link": {"$ref": "#/$defs/link"}` and one `"link": {` def block.
- Every `editor.js` hunk's surrounding context (the lines immediately before/after each hunk) matched the
  diff's context lines exactly before editing, confirmed via `Read`/`Grep` at each anchor point — i.e.
  this was not a blind patch-apply, each insertion point was individually located and confirmed absent
  beforehand.
- Confirmed every function/variable the new block references (`normalizeText`, `paragraphsToHTML`,
  `esc`, `titleString`, `safeStem`, `download`, `isMediaType`, `isMedia`, `warn`, `clearWarnings`,
  `setStatus`, `stage`, `window.uploadedMedia`, `read`) already exists elsewhere in the file, so no
  `ReferenceError` is introduced.
- Confirmed `stateFromIR()`'s new `def.link` / `e.link` reads are in scope (`def` and `e` are both
  parameters/locals already in that function, re-read in place after editing).
- `index.html`: both insertions re-read in place to confirm correct `<section>`/`</section>` nesting.

**Not verified (no execution available):** the exported `.html` file was not opened in a browser; the JS
was not run through Node; `pytest tests/canvas/` was not run. A session with real execution access
should run the existing suite and manually exercise: draw a shape, set a Link, add an image, paste rich
content (e.g. copied from a live webpage, including a hyperlink and bold/colored text) into a text body
AND into the title, click Download HTML, and open the result offline to confirm (a) the image renders
from its embedded `data:` URL, (b) the element's own `link` is clickable when safe and dropped with a
warning otherwise, (c) the pasted rich content renders as plain formatted text (bold/italic/colour
preserved via the run model) with NO surviving tags, attributes, or script execution — view-source on the
exported file and confirm no `<script>`, `on*=` attribute, or `javascript:` string appears anywhere
inside a `.txt .body` or `#title-field`.

## Recommendation to the orchestrator

This is now the fourth from-scratch rebuild of the same feature (`phase6`, `phase6_repair`,
`phase6_repair2`, this round), each starting from a freshly-provisioned `.ma-stg/stg.*` clone that does
not inherit the previous round's applied edits, because nothing durable ever lands between rounds — see
`[[[REDACTED]]]`'s "-retry/-repair sibling saying APPLIED does NOT mean the
outer tree reflects it" finding, now reconfirmed a third+ time in this exact phase family. If the
Codex Critic review step operates on the staging worktree's diff rather than on a durably-merged outer
tree, consider whether a passing review should trigger an actual merge into the outer tree's `src/`
before the next round is dispatched — otherwise every future repair round will keep re-discovering
(and re-fixing) the same already-solved problem from zero.

 1335:   function titleString() {
 1336:     var out = '';
 1337:     (function walk(node) {
 1338:       var kids = node.childNodes;
 1339:       for (var i = 0; i < kids.length; i++) {
 1340:         var child = kids[i];
 1341:         if (child.nodeType === 3) { out += child.nodeValue; continue; }

                          detect renames
    -D, --irreversible-delete
                          omit the preimage for deletes
    -C, --find-copies[=<n>]
                          detect copies
    --[no-]find-copies-harder
                          use unmodified files as source to find copies
    --no-renames          disable rename detection
    --[no-]rename-empty   use empty blobs as rename source
    --[no-]follow         continue listing the history of a file beyond renames
    -l <n>                prevent rename/copy detection if the number of rename/copy targets exceeds given limit

Diff algorithm options
    --minimal             produce the smallest possible diff
    -w, --ignore-all-space
                          ignore whitespace when comparing lines
    -b, --ignore-space-change
                          ignore changes in amount of whitespace
    --ignore-space-at-eol ignore changes in whitespace at EOL
    --ignore-cr-at-eol    ignore carrier-return at the end of line
    --ignore-blank-lines  ignore changes whose lines are all blank
    -I, --[no-]ignore-matching-lines <regex>
                          ignore changes whose all lines match <regex>
    --[no-]indent-heuristic
                          heuristic to shift diff hunk boundaries for easy reading
    --patience            generate diff using the "patience diff" algorithm
    --histogram           generate diff using the "histogram diff" algorithm
    --diff-algorithm <algorithm>
                          choose a diff algorithm
    --anchored <text>     generate diff using the "anchored diff" algorithm
    --word-diff[=<mode>]  show word diff, using <mode> to delimit changed words
    --word-diff-regex <regex>
                          use <regex> to decide what a word is
    --color-words[=<regex>]
                          equivalent to --word-diff=color --word-diff-regex=<regex>
    --[no-]color-moved[=<mode>]
                          moved lines of code are colored differently
    --[no-]color-moved-ws <mode>
                          how white spaces are ignored in --color-moved

Other diff options
    --[no-]relative[=<prefix>]
                          when run from subdir, exclude changes outside and show relative paths
    -a, --[no-]text       treat all files as text
    -R                    swap two inputs, reverse the diff
    --[no-]exit-code      exit with 1 if there were differences, 0 otherwise
    --[no-]quiet          disable all output of the program
    --[no-]ext-diff       allow an external diff helper to be executed
    --[no-]textconv       run external text conversion filters when comparing binary files
    --ignore-submodules[=<when>]
                          ignore changes to submodules in the diff generation
    --submodule[=<format>]
                          specify how differences in submodules are shown
    --ita-invisible-in-index
                          hide 'git add -N' entries from the index
    --ita-visible-in-index
                          treat 'git add -N' entries as real in the index
    -S <string>           look for differences that change the number of occurrences of the specified string
    -G <regex>            look for differences that change the number of occurrences of the specified regex
    --pickaxe-all         show all changes in the changeset with -S or -G
    --pickaxe-regex       treat <string> in -S as extended POSIX regular expression
    -O <file>             control the order in which files appear in the output
    --rotate-to <path>    show the change in the specified path first
    --skip-to <path>      skip the output to the specified path
    --find-object <object-id>
                          look for differences that change the number of occurrences of the specified object
    --diff-filter [(A|C|D|M|R|T|U|X|B)...[*]]
                          select files by diff type
    --max-depth <depth>   maximum tree depth to recurse
    --output <file>       output to a specific file
